and denote by
(E,D^{\prime})
the obtained
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM, which is
(E,D^{\prime})\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
.

Appendix H
Proof of
4.11

4.11
(recalled).

Let
R_{p}
be a parameter region and
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}(R_{p})
. Let
v\in R_{p}
.
There exists
w^{\prime}\in\mathit{TE}((E,v(D)))
iff
there exist
w\in(E,v(D))
and a delay
\delta

s.t.

w^{\prime}=w+\delta
.

Proof H.1
.

Note that this proof is inspired by
[
HRSV02
, Proof of Lemma 3.13]
. We treat first
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBMs and then
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBMs.

Claim 1
.

Let
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
. If
(E,D)
satisfies condition
3.5
(
5b
)
it has been obtained after applying
Algorithm

2

on another
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM satisfying condition
3.5
(
5a
) or a
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM.

Let
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
. If
(E,D)
satisfies condition
3.5
(
5a
)
it has been obtained after applying
Algorithm

3

on another
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM satisfying condition
3.5
(
5b
) or
after a non-parametric update applied on another
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM or a
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM.

Proof H.2
.

Let
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
and suppose
(E,D)
satisfies condition
3.5
(
5b
).
Since for all
y
, if
d_{0,y}=0
we have
\triangleleft_{0y}={<}
, from
1
and
2

it cannot be the result of a non-parametric update where there is at least a clock
x
update and
D_{x,0}=D_{0,x}=(0,\leq)
.
From
3
it cannot be the result of
Algorithm

3
,
as there must be at least a clock
x

s.t.

D_{x,0}=D_{0,x}=(0,\leq)
.
Then it is the result either from
1
of
Algorithm

2

applied on an
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM satisfying condition
3.5
(
5a
),
or from
4
of
Algorithm

2

applied on a
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM.

Let
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
and suppose
(E,D)
satisfies condition
3.5
(
5a
).
Since there is at least a clock
y

s.t.

D_{y,0}=D_{0,y}=(0,\leq)
, from
1
and
4
it cannot be the result of
Algorithm

2
,
as for all
x
, if
d_{0,x}=0
we must have
\triangleleft_{ox}={<}
.
Then it is the result of either from
3
of
Algorithm

3

applied on an
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM satisfying condition
3.5
(
5b
)
or from
1
and
2
of
Algorithm

1

applied on an
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM or a
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM.

Let
R_{p}
be a parameter region and
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}(R_{p})
.
We have to consider two different cases:
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
and
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\odot}(R_{p})
.

Claim 2
.

Let
R_{p}
be a parameter region and
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
. Let
v\in R_{p}
.
There is
w^{\prime}\in\mathit{TE}((E,v(D)))
iff
there is
w\in(E,v(D))
and a delay
\delta

s.t.

w^{\prime}=w+\delta
.

Proof H.3
.

Let
R_{p}
a parameter region and
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\blacksquare}(R_{p})
. Let
v\in R_{p}
.

•

\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM respecting
3.5
(
5a
)

Let
v\in R_{p}
. Consider
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})=\mathit{TE}((E,D))
respecting condition
3.5
(
5a
),
i. e.,

suppose there is
x_{i}

s.t.

D_{i,0}^{\prime}=-D_{0,i}^{\prime}=(0,\leq)
.
Let
w^{\prime}\in(E^{\prime},v(D^{\prime}))
, for this
x_{i}
we have
w^{\prime}(x_{i})=0
.
We need to find a value
\delta

s.t.

w^{\prime}-\delta\in(E,v(D))

which is equivalent to prove for all
x_{i},x_{j}

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ji}v(d_{j,i})

and

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ij}v(d_{i,j})

and

-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{0j}v(d_{0,j})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{j0}v(d_{j,0})\text{.}

In this proof we are going to define a
\delta
which is different from
0
,
and give it an upper bound in order to show that constraints in
(E,D)

are satisfied while going backward of
\penalty\ \delta
units of time from
w^{\prime}
.

First we will prove that for all clock
j
, its constraints of lower bound
D_{0,j}
and upper bound
D_{j,0}
are satisfied.
Second we will prove that for all
i
, bounds on their difference
D_{i,j}
and
D_{j,i}
are also satisfied.

We want to show that we have to go a little backward in time from
w^{\prime}

to ensure the upper bounds
D_{j,0}
of
(E,D)
hold.
For this purpose, we are going to prove that for all
x_{j}

D_{j,0}\leq D_{j,0}^{\prime}

is valid for
R_{p}
. Intuitively this means upper bounds of clocks in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
are greater than in
(E,D)
,
which is consistent as time is elapsing.

As
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
respects
3.5
(
5a
) and precisely
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})=\mathit{TE}_{=}((E,D))
,
we know
(E,D)
is respecting condition
3.5
(
5b
) from
1
.
As
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))=0
it was in
(E,D)
a clock with the largest fractional part,

i. e.,

x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
and
D_{i,0}=(1,<)
.

By definition of
\mathit{TE}_{<}
(cf.
Algorithm

2
), in
(E,D)
which is the
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM obtained after the application of
\mathit{TE}_{<}
on another
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM (see
1
),
for each
x_{j}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
,
D_{j,0}=D_{j,i}+(1,<)

and for all
x_{j}\in\mathbb{X}
, we have
D_{j,0}
is of the form
(d_{j,0},<)
for some
d_{j,0}
.

By definition of
\mathit{TE}_{=}
applied to
(E,D)
(cf.
Algorithm

3
), in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
, for each
x_{j}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
,

D_{j,0}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}+(1,\leq)
,
i. e.,

d_{j,0}=d_{j,0}^{\prime}
.
Hence by
3.3
(
2b
) and as
\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}
is either
\leq
or
<
, we have

(d_{j,0},<)=D_{j,0}\leq D_{j,0}^{\prime}=(d_{j,0},\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}})

is valid for
R_{p}
.
Next we define the largest amount of time so that all upper bounds of
(E,D)
are satisfied.

We claim that for all
x_{j}
,
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-v(d_{j,0})\leq 0
.

Indeed, remark that by applying
Algorithm

2
then 3,
constraints on upper bounds of clocks
in
(E,D)
and
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
differ only by their
\triangleleft
. As for
i\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)

and
j\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
it we have
D_{j,0}=D_{j,i}+(1,<)
in
(E,D)

and
D_{j,0}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}+(1,\leq)
in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
, so
d_{j,0}=d_{j,0}^{\prime}
.
Since for any
x
, its fractional part is less or equal to its upper bound in
D
and therefore in
D^{\prime}
,
any difference between a fractional part and its upper bound is either negative or null.
For all
x
, since
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))\triangleleft_{x0^{\prime}}v(d_{x,0}^{\prime})

we have
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))-v(d_{x,0}^{\prime})\triangleleft_{x0^{\prime}}0
.
Since
v(d_{x,0}^{\prime})=v(d_{x,0})
,
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))-v(d_{x,0})\triangleleft_{x0^{\prime}}0
,
therefore we have our result.

Now we claim that we have to go at least an
\epsilon>0
backward in time to ensure all bounds of
(E,D)
are met.
Let
x_{j}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
. As

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}v(d_{j,0})

we have

–

either
\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}={<}
and we already have

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))<v(d_{j,0})
,

–

or
\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}={\leq}
and for any
\epsilon>0

we have
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\epsilon<v(d_{j,0})
.

It is also true for each
x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
: after applying
\mathit{TE}_{<}
recall that we have
D_{i,0}=(1,<)
.
We can take
\epsilon>0
and define
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))=1-\epsilon
,
so we have
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))<v(d_{i,0})
.

Now that we know we have to go a little backward in time (at least an
\epsilon>0
)
so upper bounds of
(E,D)
are satisfied, we are going to give an upper bound to
\epsilon

so that all lower bounds
D_{0,j}
of
(E,D)
are also satisfied.

Let

t_{1}=\min\limits_{x\in\mathbb{X}}\{\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))+v(d_{0,x})\}

We want to prove that
t_{1}>0
.

Let us prove that for all
x_{j}
,
D_{0,j}^{\prime}\leq D_{0,j}
is valid for
R_{p}
.
Recall that for
x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
, we have that
D_{i,0}=(1,<)
.
Moreover, from
3.5
(
4
)
D_{i,j}\leq D_{i,0}+D_{0,j}
is valid for
R_{p}
, then we have

D_{i,j}\leq(1,<)+D_{0,j}

is valid for
R_{p}
.
Recall that after applying
Algorithm

3
,

D_{0,j}^{\prime}=D_{i,j}+(-1,\leq)
.
By
3.3
(
2b
) we have
(-1,\leq)\leq(-1,\leq)
.
We invoke
3.4
which gives

D_{i,j}+(-1,\leq)\leq(1,<)+D_{0,j}+(-1,\leq)=D_{0,j}+(0,<)\text{ is valid for\penalty\ $R_{p}${}.}\quad

(19)

As, from
3.3
(
2b
) we have
D_{0,j}+(0,<)\leq D_{0,j}
is valid for
R_{p}
,
we infer (
19
) and it gives

D_{0,j}^{\prime}\leq D_{0,j}\text{ is valid for\penalty\ $R_{p}${}.}

Since
w^{\prime}\in(E^{\prime},v(D^{\prime}))
we have
-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})
,

0\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})\text{.}

Then we have that

0\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})\leq\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})

where,

–

either from
3.3
(
2a
)
d_{0,j}^{\prime}<d_{0,j}
;

–

or from
3.3
(
2b
),
d_{0,j}^{\prime}\leq d_{0,j}

and then
\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{0j}={<}
.
Indeed as
D_{0,j}^{\prime}\leq D_{0,j}
is valid for
R_{p}
, and since
(E,D)
is the

\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM obtained after the application of
\mathit{TE}_{<}
(cf.
Algorithm

2
) on another
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM (see
1
),
we have
\triangleleft_{0j}={<}
.

To conclude we have that for all
x_{j}
either

0\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})<\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})

or

0<\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})\leq\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})\text{.}

As
t_{1}
is by definition the minimum value of an expression
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})
for a given
x_{j}
,
which as we just proved are all strictly positive, we have that for all
x_{j}

0<t_{1}\leq\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})\text{.}

We proved that
t_{1}>0
,
so we can set
\delta=\frac{t_{1}}{2}
(therefore
\delta>0
).

More intuitively
\delta
is the value right in the middle of the least and the largest amount of time

s.t.
we can go backward in time from
w^{\prime}
and respect all constraints defined in
(E,v(D))
.

Now we are going to prove that for any clock
x_{j}
, its constraints on lower and upper bounds are satisfied,
i. e.,

-v(d_{0,j})\triangleleft_{0j}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{j0}v(d_{j,0})\text{.}

First as
\delta<t_{1}
, we have

-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta<-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+t_{1}\leq-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})=v(d_{0,j})

which is
-v(d_{0,j})<\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta
.
Since
(E,D)
is the
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM obtained after the application of
\mathit{TE}_{<}
(cf.
Algorithm

3
) on another
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM (see
1
),
we have
\triangleleft_{0j}={<}
so
-v(d_{0,j})\triangleleft_{0j}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta
.
Secondary as
0<\delta
, we have

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta<\mathit{frac}(w)^{\prime}(x_{j})-0\leq\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{j,0})=v(d_{j,0})

which is
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta<v(d_{j,0})
.
Since
(E,D)
is the
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM obtained after the application of
\mathit{TE}_{<}
(cf.
Algorithm

3
) on another
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM (see
1
),
we have
\triangleleft_{j0}={<}
so
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{j0}v(d_{j,0})

Now we prove that constraints defined in
(E,D)
on differences of clocks are also satisfied
by going back of
\delta
units of time from
w^{\prime}
.

Recall that in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
we have for all clock
x_{j}
,

D_{j,i}^{\prime}=D_{j,0}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}+1\quad\text{and}\quad D_{i,j}^{\prime}=D_{0,j}^{\prime}=-1+D_{i,j}\text{.}

In addition by definition of
\mathit{TE}_{=}
, for
x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
,
E_{x_{i}}=E_{x_{i}}^{\prime}-1

and for
x_{j}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
,
E_{x_{j}}=E_{x_{j}}^{\prime}
.

We already treated the case whether
i
or
j
are
0
, now suppose
i,j
are both different from
0
.

–

if
x_{i},x_{j}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
: let
x\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
and
recall that after applying
Algorithm

3
,

D_{i,j}^{\prime}=D_{i,j}
,
D_{j,i}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}
;
we have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}d_{j,i}^{\prime}=d_{j,i}
,
and therefore
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ji}d_{j,i}
.

We also have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}d_{i,j}^{\prime}=d_{i,j}
,
therefore
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ij}d_{i,j}
;

–

if
x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
and
x_{j}\in\mathbb{X}\setminus\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
:
recall that after applying
Algorithm

3
,

D_{j,0}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}+(1,\leq)
, and
D_{0,j}^{\prime}=D_{i,j}+(-1,\leq)
.
Observe that as we added
\leq
which is the neutral element of the addition
\oplus
between two operators
\triangleleft
,
we have
\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ji}
and
\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ij}
.
Note that as
x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
, in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
we have
D_{0,i}^{\prime}=(0,\leq)=D_{i,0}^{\prime}

which means
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))=0
.
Going backward in time of
\delta
units of time from
w^{\prime}(x_{i})

means that
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))=1-\delta
.

We have that

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}v(d_{j,0}^{\prime})=v(d_{j,i})+1

hence
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-1\triangleleft_{ji}v(d_{j,i})
which is equivalent to

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta-1+\delta\triangleleft_{ji}v(d_{j,i})\text{.}

The same way we have

-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})=v(d_{i,j})-1

hence
1-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{ij}v(d_{i,j})
which is equivalent to

1-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ij}v(d_{i,j})\text{.}

To conclude, we define for all
x_{j}

s.t.

D_{0,j}^{\prime}\neq(0,\leq)
and
D_{j,0}^{\prime}\neq(0,\leq)

w(x_{j})=w^{\prime}(x_{j})-\delta

and for all
x_{i}

s.t.

D_{0,i}^{\prime}=(0,\leq)=D_{i,0}^{\prime}

w(x_{i})=(w^{\prime}(x_{i})-1)+1-\delta

and clearly,
w\in(E,v(D))
.

•

\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM respecting
3.5
(
5b
)

Let
v\in R_{p}
.
Consider
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})=\mathit{TE}((E,D))
respecting condition
3.5
(
5b
),
i. e.,

suppose there is at least an
x_{i}

s.t.

D_{i,0}^{\prime}=(1,<)

and for all
j

s.t.

D_{0,j}=(0,\triangleleft_{0j})
, then we have
{\triangleleft_{0j}}={<}
.
Let
w^{\prime}\in(E^{\prime},v(D^{\prime}))
.

We need to find a value
\delta

s.t.

w^{\prime}-\delta\in(E,v(D))

which is equivalent to prove for all
x_{i},x_{j}

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ji}v(d_{j,i})

and

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ij}v(d_{i,j})

and

-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{0j}v(d_{0,j})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{j0}v(d_{j,0})\text{.}

As done previously we are going to define a
\delta
which is different from
0
so we satisfy condition
3.5
(
5a
),
and show that constraints in
(E,D)

are satisfied while going backward of
\delta
units of time from
w^{\prime}
.

We define the largest and the least amount of time so that all upper bounds of
(E,D)
are satisfied.
Let

t_{0}=\max\limits_{x\in\mathbb{X}}\{0,\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))-v(d_{x,0})\}

and

t_{1}=\min\limits_{x\in\mathbb{X}}\{\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))+v(d_{0,x})\}\text{.}

We want to prove that
t_{0}=t_{1}>0
.
For this purpose, let us first show that for all
i,j

we have
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-v(d_{j,0}^{\prime})\leq\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+v(d_{0,i}^{\prime})
,
which is
t_{0}\leq t_{1}
.

First note that for all
i,j

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}v(d_{j,i}^{\prime})\text{.}

By applying
\mathit{TE}_{<}
(
Algorithm

2
)
to
(E,D)
, we have that
D_{j,i}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}
,
i. e.,

(d_{i,j},\triangleleft_{ij})=(d_{i,j}^{\prime},\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}})
,
and from
3.5
(
4
) we have that
D_{j,i}\leq D_{j,0}+D_{0,i}
is valid for
R_{p}
.

Hence, we have from
3.3
(
2b
) that either
v(d_{j,i})<v(d_{j,0})+v(d_{0,i})
or

v(d_{j,i})\leq v(d_{j,0})+v(d_{0,i})
and
\triangleleft_{ji}=\triangleleft_{j0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0i}
or
\triangleleft_{ji}={<}
and
\triangleleft_{j0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0i}={\leq}
.

We can then write that

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))(\triangleleft_{j0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0i})v(d_{j,0})+v(d_{0,i})

which is equivalent to

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-v(d_{j,0})(\triangleleft_{j0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0i})\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+v(d_{0,i})

so we obtain our result, as
(\triangleleft_{j0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0i})
is either
\leq
or
<
.

Now, recall that
(E,D)
respects condition
3.5
(
5a
)
so we have at least an
x

s.t.

D_{x,0}=D_{0,x}=(0,\leq)
.

For this clock
x
we have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))=\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))-v(d_{x,0})\leq t_{0}

and that
t_{1}\leq\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))+v(d_{0,x})=\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))
.

Hence
t_{0}=t_{1}=\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))
.

As
\triangleleft_{x0}={\leq}
,
we have
(\triangleleft_{x0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0i})=\triangleleft_{0i}

and
(\triangleleft_{j0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0x})=\triangleleft_{j0}
, which gives

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))=\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))-v(d_{x,0})\triangleleft_{0i}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+v(d_{0,i})

and

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-v(d_{j,0})\triangleleft_{j0}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))+v(d_{0,x})=\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))\text{.}

Moreover in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
we have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))\triangleleft_{0x^{\prime}}v(d_{0,x}^{\prime})
.
Since
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
respects condition
3.5
(
5b
), if
D_{0,x}^{\prime}=(0,\triangleleft_{0x^{\prime}})
then

\triangleleft_{0x^{\prime}}={<}
.
Hence
0<\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))
and

0<t_{0}=t_{1}\text{.}

Let
\delta=t_{0}=t_{1}
.
More intuitively
\delta
is the value right in the middle of the least and the largest amount of time

s.t.
we can go backward in time from
w^{\prime}
and respect all constraints defined in
(E,v(D))
.

First we have

-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\leq-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+t_{1}\triangleleft_{j0}-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})=v(d_{0,j})

which is
-v(d_{0,j})\triangleleft_{j0}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta
.

Secondary we have

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\leq\mathit{frac}(w)^{\prime}(x_{j})-t_{0}\triangleleft_{0j}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{j,0})=v(d_{j,0})

which is
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{0j}v(d_{j,0})
.

Now we prove that constraints defined in
(E,D)
on differences of clocks are also satisfied
by going back of
\delta
units of time from
w^{\prime}

Recall that in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
from the definition of
Algorithm

2

we have for all clocks
x_{i},x_{j}
,

D_{j,i}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}\quad\text{and}\quad D_{i,j}^{\prime}=D_{i,j}\text{.}

Since we already treated the case whether
i
or
j
are
0
, now suppose
i,j
are both different from
0
.
We have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}v(d_{j,i}^{\prime})=v(d_{j,i})
,
and therefore as
\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ji}
,

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ji}v(d_{j,i})\text{.}

We also have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}v(d_{i,j}^{\prime})=v(d_{i,j})
,
therefore as
\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ij}
,

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ij}v(d_{i,j})\text{.}

To conclude, we define for all
x_{j}

w(x_{j})=w^{\prime}(x_{j})-\delta

and clearly,
w\in(E,v(D))
.

Conversely, let

Claim 3
.

Let
R_{p}
be a parameter region and
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\odot}(R_{p})

and a delay

.

.

)

{\triangleleft_{0j}}={<}

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))-v(d_{x,0})\leq t_{0}

and that
t_{1}\leq\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))+v(d_{0,x})=\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))-v(d_{x,0})
.

Hence
t_{0}=t_{1}
.

As for all
x
,
\triangleleft_{x0}={\leq}
,
we have for all
i,j
that
(\triangleleft_{x0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0i})=\triangleleft_{0i}

and
(\triangleleft_{j0}\oplus\triangleleft_{0x})=\triangleleft_{j0}
, which gives

t_{1}\triangleleft_{0i}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+v(d_{0,i})

and

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-v(d_{j,0})\triangleleft_{j0}t_{0}\text{.}

Moreover in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
we have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))\triangleleft_{0x^{\prime}}v(d_{0,x}^{\prime})
.
From
1
,
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
is obtained after applying
Algorithm

2

and therefore
\triangleleft_{0x^{\prime}}={<}
.
Hence
0<\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x))
and

0<t_{0}=t_{1}\text{.}

Let
\delta=t_{0}=t_{1}
.
More intuitively
\delta
is the value right in the middle of the least and the largest amount of time

s.t.
we can go backward in time from
w^{\prime}
and respect all constraints defined in
(E,v(D))
.

First we have

-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\leq-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+t_{1}\triangleleft_{j0}-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j})=v(d_{0,j})

which is
-v(d_{0,j})\triangleleft_{j0}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta
.

Secondary we have

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\leq\mathit{frac}(w)^{\prime}(x_{j})-t_{0}\triangleleft_{0j}\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+v(d_{j,0})=v(d_{j,0})

which is
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{0j}v(d_{j,0})
.

Now we prove that constraints defined in
(E,D)
on differences of clocks are also satisfied
by going back of
\delta
units of time from
w^{\prime}

Recall that in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
from the definition of
Algorithm

2

we have for all clocks
x_{i},x_{j}
,

D_{j,i}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}\quad\text{and}\quad D_{i,j}^{\prime}=D_{i,j}\text{.}

Since we already treated the case whether
i
or
j
are
0
, now suppose
i,j
are both different from
0
.
We have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}v(d_{j,i}^{\prime})=v(d_{j,i})
,
and therefore as
\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ji}
,

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ji}v(d_{j,i})\text{.}

We also have that
\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}v(d_{i,j}^{\prime})=v(d_{i,j})
,
therefore as
\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ij}
,

\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{i}))-\delta-\mathit{frac}(w^{\prime}(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{ij}v(d_{i,j})\text{.}

To conclude, we define for all
x_{j}

w(x_{j})=w^{\prime}(x_{j})-\delta

and clearly,
w\in(E,v(D))
.

(
\Longrightarrow
)
for
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBMs

Assume in
(E,D)\in p\textsf{--}\mathcal{PDBM}_{\odot}(R_{p})
.
Let
v\in R_{p}
, and
x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
.

Now consider
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})=\mathit{TE}((E,D))
.
We need to find a value
\delta

s.t.

w+\delta\in(E^{\prime},v(D^{\prime}))
.
which is equivalent to prove for all
x_{i},x_{j}

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+\delta-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))-\delta\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}v(d_{j,i}^{\prime})

and

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))+\delta-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}v(d_{i,j}^{\prime})

and

-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})\quad\text{and}\quad\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}v(d_{j,0}^{\prime})\text{.}

As done previously we are going to define a
\delta
which is different from
0
,
and show that constraints in
(E,D)

are satisfied while going forward of
\delta
units of time from
w
.

Let

t_{0}=\max\limits_{x\in\mathbb{X}}\{0,-\mathit{frac}(w(x))-\mathit{frac}(v(d_{0,x}^{\prime}))\}

and

t_{1}=\min\limits_{x\in\mathbb{X}}\{\mathit{frac}(v(d_{x,0}^{\prime}))-\mathit{frac}(w(x))\}\text{.}

We prove that
t_{1}\leq t_{0}
.

for any clock
i
we have that
D_{i,0}=(\mathit{frac}(p),\leq)
and
D_{i,0}=(-\mathit{frac}(p),\leq)

i. e.,

d_{0,i}=-d_{i,0}

for some
p
, hence
-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))-v(d_{0,i})=-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))+v(d_{i,0})
.

By definition of
\mathit{TE}_{<}
applied to
(E,D)
, we have that
D_{0,i}^{\prime}=D_{0,i}+(0,<)
.
In order to respect the constraint
-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))-\delta\triangleleft_{0i^{\prime}}v(d_{0,i}^{\prime})

which is, as
\triangleleft_{0i^{\prime}}=<
,
-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))-\delta<v(d_{0,i}^{\prime})
,
we have to find a
\delta>0
.

In order to find an upper bound for
\delta
, we are going to prove that
t_{1}>0
.
From
3.6
(
2
) we have in
(E,D)
that for any clocks
i,j

D_{j,0}\leq D_{j,i}+D_{i,0}
. Let
x_{i}\in\mathsf{LFP}_{R_{p}}(D)
.
From
3.6
(
1
), we have that
D_{i,0}\leq(1,<)
.
This gives that
D_{j,i}+D_{i,0}\leq D_{j,i}+(1,<)
.

By definition of
\mathit{TE}_{<}
applied to
(E,D)
, we have that
D_{j,0}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}+(1,<)
.
Hence we have
D_{j,0}\leq D_{j,0}^{\prime}
.

Now as
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))\triangleleft_{i0}v(d_{i,0})

we can write
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))\triangleleft_{i0^{\prime}}v(d_{i,0}^{\prime})

and then
0\triangleleft_{i0^{\prime}}v(d_{i,0}^{\prime})-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))

where
\triangleleft_{i0^{\prime}}=<
, which prove our result.

We define
\delta=\frac{t_{1}}{2}
, therefore
t_{0}<\delta<t_{1}
.
Now for all clock
x_{j}
, we obtain two results.
First we have

-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))-\delta<-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))-t_{1}\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})=v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})

which is
-v(d_{0,j}^{\prime})\triangleleft_{0j}\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+\delta
as
\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}={<}
.

Secondary we have

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+\delta<\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+t_{0}\triangleleft_{j0^{\prime}}\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+v(d_{j,0}^{\prime})=v(d_{j,0}^{\prime})

which is
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+\delta\triangleleft_{j0}v(d_{j,0}^{\prime})
as
\triangleleft_{0j^{\prime}}={<}
.

Now we prove that constraints defined in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
on differences of clocks are also satisfied
by going forward of
\delta
units of time from
w

Recall that in
(E^{\prime},D^{\prime})
from the definition of
Algorithm

2
we have for all clock
x_{j}
,

D_{j,i}^{\prime}=D_{j,i}\quad\text{and}\quad D_{i,j}^{\prime}=D_{i,j}\text{.}

Since we already treated the case whether
i
or
j
are
0
, now suppose
i,j
are both different from
0
.
We have that
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))\triangleleft_{ji}v(d_{j,i})=v(d_{j,i}^{\prime})
,
and therefore as
\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ji}
,

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))+\delta-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))-\delta\triangleleft_{ji^{\prime}}v(d_{j,i}^{\prime})\text{.}

We also have that
\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))\triangleleft_{ij}v(d_{i,j})=v(d_{i,j}^{\prime})
,
therefore as
\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}=\triangleleft_{ij}
,

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))+\delta-\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))-\delta\triangleleft_{ij^{\prime}}v(d_{i,j}^{\prime})\text{.}

Finally, we define
w^{\prime}=w+\delta
and
w^{\prime}\in(E^{\prime},v(D^{\prime}))
.

Appendix I
Proof of
4.12

4.12
(recalled).

Let
(E,D)
be a
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM for
R_{p}
and
v\in R_{p}
.
Let
g
be a non-parametric guard.
If
v\in\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E,D)
, then for all
v^{\prime}\in R_{p}
,
v^{\prime}\in\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E,D)
.

Proof I.1
.

Our idea is to define a clock region “larger” than our
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM (following
2.3
) and show that, even for this (larger) clock region, either all clock valuations satisfy the guard—or none does.

Definition 1
.

Let
R_{p}
be a parameter region,
v\in R_{p}
. Let
(E,D)
be a
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM for
R_{p}
.
We define the
clock region containing
(E,v(D))
, denoted by
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
, as follows:
for all
w\in{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
, for all clocks
x_{i},x_{j}
,

•

if
E_{x_{i}}<K
,
\lfloor w(x_{i})\rfloor=E_{x_{i}}
,
else if
E_{x_{i}}=\infty
,
w(x_{i})\geq K
;

•

if
(0,\leq)<D_{i,j}
is valid for
R_{p}
and
E_{x_{i}}<K
,

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))<\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))
;

•

if
(0,\leq)=D_{i,j}
is valid for
R_{p}
and
E_{x_{i}}<K
,

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{j}))=\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))
;

•

if
D_{i,0}=D_{0,i}=(0,\leq)
and
E_{x_{i}}<K
,

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))=0
;

•

if
D_{i,0}\neq(0,\leq),D_{0,i}\neq(0,\leq)
and
E_{x_{i}}<K
,

\mathit{frac}(w(x_{i}))\neq 0
.

Claim 2
.

Let
(E,D)
be a
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM for
R_{p}
and
v\in R_{p}
.
We have
(E,v(D))\subseteq{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.

Proof I.2
.

Clock regions of
2.3
define constraints on clocks of the form
0=\mathit{frac}(x)
,

0<\mathit{frac}(x)<1
,
0=\mathit{frac}(x)-\mathit{frac}(y)
and
0<\mathit{frac}(x)-\mathit{frac}(y)<1
for some
x,y
,
and
\lfloor x\rfloor=k
for some integer
k
.
Let
(E,D)
be a
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM for
R_{p}
and
v\in R_{p}
. It defines constraints

\bigwedge_{i,j\in{[0,H]}^{2}}\mathit{frac}(x_{i})-\mathit{frac}(x_{j})\triangleleft_{i,j}v(d_{i,j})\quad\land\quad\bigwedge_{i\in[1,H]}\lfloor x_{i}\rfloor=E_{i}\textit{.}

Clearly, if
w\in(E,v(D))
satisfies
\lfloor x_{i}\rfloor=E_{i}
then it satisfies the same constraint defined in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.

Consider the constraints
\mathit{frac}(x_{i})-\mathit{frac}(x_{j})\triangleleft_{i,j}v(d_{i,j})
and
\mathit{frac}(x_{j})-\mathit{frac}(x_{i})\triangleleft_{j,i}v(d_{j,i})
.

•

Suppose
i,j
are both different from
0
. From
3.5
(
3
) and
3.6
,
either
d_{i,j}=d_{j,i}
and then
\triangleleft_{i,j}={\leq}=\triangleleft_{j,i}
, then if
d_{i,j}=d_{j,i}=0
it satisfies the same constraint defined in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
,
or
d_{i,j}
and
d_{j,i}
are different from
0
, as they are elements of
\mathcal{PLT}
which are strictly smaller than
1
, it satisfies either
0<\mathit{frac}(x_{i})-\mathit{frac}(x_{j})<1

or
0=\mathit{frac}(x_{i})-\mathit{frac}(x_{j})
in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.
Finally if
d_{i,j}\neq d_{j,i}
, then
\triangleleft_{i,j}={<}=\triangleleft_{j,i}
and it satisfies
0<\mathit{frac}(x_{i})-\mathit{frac}(x_{j})<1
in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.

•

Suppose
i
is different from
0
and
j=0
.
From
3.5
(
3
) and
3.6
,
either
d_{i,0}=d_{0,i}
and then
\triangleleft_{i,0}={\leq}=\triangleleft_{0,i}
, then if
d_{i,0}=d_{0,i}=0
it satisfies the same constraint defined in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
,
or
d_{i,0}
and
d_{0,i}
are different from
0
, as they are elements of
\mathcal{PLT}
which are strictly smaller than
1
, it satisfies either
0<\mathit{frac}(x_{i})<1

or
0=\mathit{frac}(x_{i})
in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.
Finally if
d_{i,0}\neq d_{0,i}
, then
\triangleleft_{i,0}={<}=\triangleleft_{0,i}
and it satisfies
0<\mathit{frac}(x_{i})<1
in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.

•

The case
j
is different from
0
and
i=0
is similar.

•

Suppose both
i,j
are
0
, the constraint is not taken into account as we have no
x_{0}
in
{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.

Finally, we have that if
w\in(E,v(D))
then
w\in{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.

Now we come back to the proof of the main lemma:

Let
(E,D)
be a
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM for
R_{p}
and
v\in R_{p}
. It defines constraints

\bigwedge_{i,j\in{[0,H]}^{2}}\mathit{frac}(x_{i})-\mathit{frac}(x_{j})\triangleleft_{i,j}v(d_{i,j})\quad\land\quad\bigwedge_{i\in[1,H]}\lfloor x_{i}\rfloor=E_{i}\textit{.}

Moreover, let
g
be a non-parametric guard. It defines constraints for a finite number of integer constants
k_{i}
with
i\in I\subseteq[1,H]

\bigwedge_{i\in I}\mathit{frac}(x_{i})\leq 0\quad\land\quad\bigwedge_{i\in I}-\mathit{frac}(x_{i})\leq 0\quad\land\quad\bigwedge_{i\in I}\lfloor x_{i}\rfloor\bowtie k_{i}\textit{.}

The intersection between the two is given by the conjunction of those constraints.
We project this intersection on parameter variables (by elimination of clock variables) and we prove that the intersection does not create new constraints on parameters different from those we already have in
(E,v(D))
(and therefore in
R_{p}
).
For some set of clocks
I\subseteq[1,H]
and
i\in I
,
suppose we have the constraints
\mathit{frac}(x_{i})\leq 0
and
-\mathit{frac}(x_{i})\leq 0
in
g
.
When eliminating
x_{i}
in any constraint of the form
\mathit{frac}(x_{i})-\mathit{frac}(x_{j})\triangleleft_{i,j}v(d_{i,j})
,
it is clear that we proceed on
\mathcal{PLT}
to the operation
(0,\leq)+(d_{i,j},\triangleleft_{i,j})=(0+d_{i,j},\leq\oplus\triangleleft_{i,j})=(d_{i,j},\triangleleft_{i,j})
.
The same way on any constraint of the form
\mathit{frac}(x_{i})\triangleleft_{i,0}v(d_{i,0})
, eliminating
x_{i}

gives the constraint
(0,\leq)+(d_{i,0},\triangleleft_{i,0})=(d_{i,0},\triangleleft_{i,0})
.
Hence it does not create new inequalities not belonging to
R_{p}
.

Now suppose
v\in\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E,D)
. We have that all
w\in(E,v(D))
satisfy
g
.

As no new constraints not in
\mathcal{PLT}
have been created, all
v^{\prime}\in R_{p}
respect the same constraints on their fractional part and integer part as
v

and therefore,
(E,v^{\prime}(D))
is contained in the same clock region as
(E,v(D))
is,

i. e.,

{[(E,v(D))]}_{R_{c}}={[(E,v^{\prime}(D))]}_{R_{c}}
.
Finally,
v^{\prime}\in\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E,D)
.

Appendix J
Proof of
4.13

4.13
(recalled).

Let
(E,D)
be a
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM for
R_{p}
and
v\in R_{p}
.
Let
g
be a parametric guard.
If
v\in\mathit{p\mbox{-}guard}_{\exists}(g,E,D)
, then for all
v^{\prime}\in R_{p}
,
v^{\prime}\in\mathit{p\mbox{-}guard}_{\exists}(g,E,D)
.

Proof J.1
.

Let
(E,D)
be a
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM for
R_{p}
and
v\in R_{p}
.
Let
g
be a parametric guard and suppose
v\in\mathit{p\mbox{-}guard}_{\exists}(g,E,D)
.
After applying a projection on parameters, we obtain constraints on elements of
\mathcal{PLT}
.
By hypothesis, all these constraints are satisfied by
v
.
Suppose
v^{\prime}\in R_{p}
.
By definition of our parameter regions, and since
v
and
v^{\prime}
both belong to
R_{p}
,
v^{\prime}
satisfies the same constraints on elements of
\mathcal{PLT}
.
Therefore, the same constraints is satisfied by
v^{\prime}
and
v^{\prime}\in\mathit{p\mbox{-}guard}_{\exists}(g,E,D)
.

Appendix K
Proof of
6.1

6.1
(recalled).

Let
R_{p}
be a parameter region. Let
\mathcal{A}
be an
R-U2P-PTA
and
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})
its parametric region automaton over
R_{p}
.
There is a run
\sigma:(\ell_{0},(E_{0},D{}_{0}))\overset{e_{0}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{1},(E{}_{1},D{}_{1}))\overset{e_{1}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots(\ell_{f-1},(E{}_{f-1},D{}_{f-1}))\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},(E{}_{f},D{}_{f}))

in
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})
iff for all
v\in R_{p}
there is a run
\rho:(\ell_{0},w_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{0}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\ell_{1},w_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}\cdots(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{f-1}}}{{\longrightarrow}}(\ell_{f},w_{f})

in
v(\mathcal{A})

s.t.
for all
0\leq i\leq f
,
w_{i}\in(E_{i},v(D_{i}))
.

Proof K.1
.

\Leftarrow

By induction on the length of the run.

Let
v\in R_{p}
.
As the basis for the induction, in the initial location
(\ell{}_{0},{\{0\}}^{H})
the only valuation is reachable by an empty run of
v(\mathcal{A})
.
Moreover
{\{0\}}^{H}{\in}(E_{0},v(D_{0}))
the initial
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM containing only
0
.
Therefore the initial location

(\ell{}_{0},(E_{0},v(D_{0})))
is reachable by an empty run of
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})
.

For the induction step, suppose for all
v
, there is run in
v(\mathcal{A})
of length
f-1
we have our result.

Let
v\in R_{p}
and
\rho=(\ell_{0},w_{0})\overset{e_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{e_{f-2}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},w_{f})
be a run of
v(\mathcal{A})
of length
f
.
By induction hypothesis, there is a run
\sigma=(\ell_{0},(E_{0},D_{0}))\overset{e_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{e_{f-2}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f-1},(E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))
in
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})
and for all
0\leq i\leq f-1
,
w_{i}\in(E_{i},v(D_{i}))
.

Consider
e_{f-1}
. By
5.1
of the parametric region automaton, it is also in its set of edges
\zeta^{\prime}
.
Three cases show up:

–

If
e_{f-1}=\langle\ell_{f-1},a,g,u_{np},\ell_{f}\rangle
contains no parametric guard nor parametric update.
Using
2.2
there is a delay
\delta
(possibly
0
)
s.t.

(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta}}{{\mapsto}}(\ell_{f-1}w_{f-1}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{f-1}}}{{\mapsto}}(\ell_{f},w_{f})
where
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\models g
and
w_{f}={[w_{f-1}^{\prime}]}_{u_{np}}
.
As
w_{f-1}\in(E_{f-1},v(D_{f-1}))
there is
(E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{Succ}((E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))

s.t.
from
4.11
we have
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\in(E_{f-1}^{\prime},v(D_{f-1}^{\prime}))
.
As
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\models g
by construction of our
\mathsf{p}
–PDBMs (see
Section

4.4
) any other clock valuation belonging to
(E_{f-1}^{\prime},v(D_{f-1}^{\prime}))
satisfies
g
.
Therefore
v\in\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})
and from
4.12
,
R_{p}\subseteq\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})
.
Now, as
w_{f}={[w_{f-1}^{\prime}]}_{u_{np}}
consider the
\mathsf{open}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM
(E_{f},D_{f})=\mathit{update}((E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime}),u_{np})
;
from
4.4
we have
w_{f}\in(E_{f},v(D_{f}))
.
Finally there is an edge

(\ell_{f-1},(E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},(E_{f},D_{f})).

–

If
e_{f-1}=\langle\ell_{f-1},a,g,u,\ell_{f}\rangle
contains a parametric guard and a parametric update.
Using
2.2
there is a delay
\delta
(possibly
0
)
s.t.

(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\delta}}{{\mapsto}}(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1}^{\prime})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle e_{f-1}}}{{\mapsto}}(\ell_{f},w_{f})
where
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\models v(g)
and
w_{f}={[w_{f-1}^{\prime}]}_{v(u)}
.
As
w_{f-1}\in(E_{f-1},v(D_{f-1}))
there is
(E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{Succ}((E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))

s.t.
from
4.11
we have
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\in(E_{f-1}^{\prime},v(D_{f-1}^{\prime}))
.
As
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\models v(g)
,
v\in\mathit{p\mbox{-}guard}_{\exists}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})
and from
4.13
,
R_{p}\subseteq\mathit{p\mbox{-}guard}_{\exists}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})
.
Now, as
w_{f}={[w_{f-1}^{\prime}]}_{v(u)}
consider the
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM
(E_{f},D_{f})=\overline{\mathit{update}}((E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime}),u)
;

(E_{f},v(D_{f}))
contains only one clock valuation, precisely defined by the fully parametric update
v(u)
so we have
w_{f}\in(E_{f},v(D_{f}))
.
Finally there is an edge
(\ell_{f-1},(E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},(E_{f},D_{f}))
.

–

The case where
e_{f-1}
contains a non parametric guard and a parametric update is similar to the previous one.

Finally, there is a run
\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},(E_{f},D_{f}))
of length
f
in
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})

s.t.
for all
0\leq i\leq f
,
w_{i}\in(E_{i},v(D_{i}))
.

\Rightarrow

By induction on the length of the run.

Let
v\in R_{p}
.
As the basis for the induction, the initial location
(\ell{}_{0},(E_{0},v(D_{0})))
is reachable by an empty run of
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})
.
Moreover, as
{\{0\}}^{H}{\in}(E_{0},v(D_{0}))
, the initial location

(\ell{}_{0},{\{0\}}^{H})
is reachable by an empty run of
v(\mathcal{A})
.

For the induction step, suppose it is true for all run in
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})
of length
f-1
.

Let
v\in R_{p}
and
\sigma=(\ell_{0},(E_{0},D_{0}))\overset{e_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{e_{f-2}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f-1},(E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},(E_{f},D_{f}))
be a run of
\mathcal{R}(\mathcal{A})
of length
f
.
Consider
e_{f-1}
. By
5.1
of the parametric region automaton, it is also in the set of edges
\zeta
of
\mathcal{A}
.
Two cases show up:

–

If
e_{f-1}=\langle\ell_{f-1},a,g,u_{np},\ell_{f}\rangle
contains no parametric guard nor parametric update.
By induction hypothesis, there is a run
\rho=(\ell_{0},w_{0})\overset{e_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{e_{f-2}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})
of
v(\mathcal{A})
of length
f-1

s.t.
for all
0\leq i\leq f-1
,
w_{i}\in(E_{i},v(D_{i}))
.
Using
5.1
there is
(E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{Succ}((E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))
,

R_{p}\subseteq\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})
and

(E_{f},D_{f})=\mathit{update}((E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime}),u_{np})
.
From
4.11
we have
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\in(E_{f-1}^{\prime},v(D_{f-1}^{\prime}))
and a delay
\delta

s.t.

w_{f-1}^{\prime}=w_{f-1}+\delta
.
As
R_{p}\subseteq\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})

from
4.12
we have
v\in\mathit{guard}_{\forall}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})
and
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\models g
.
Moreover, since
(E_{f},D_{f})=\mathit{update}((E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime}),u_{np})
, we define
w_{f}={[w_{f-1}^{\prime}]}_{u_{np}}

and therefore from
4.4
,
w_{f}\in(E_{f},v(D_{f}))
.
Finally there is an edge
(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},w_{f})
and a run
\rho^{\prime}=\rho\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},w_{f})
in
v(\mathcal{A})
of length
f

s.t.
for all
0\leq i\leq f
,
w_{i}\in(E_{i},v(D_{i}))
.

–

If
e_{f-1}=\langle\ell_{f-1},a,g,u,\ell_{f}\rangle
contains a parametric guard and a parametric update.
Using
5.1
there is
(E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})\in\mathsf{Succ}((E_{f-1},D_{f-1}))
,

R_{p}\subseteq\mathit{p\mbox{-}guard}_{\exists}(g,E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime})
and

(E_{f},D_{f})=\overline{\mathit{update}}((E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime}),u)
.
From
4.13
we can take
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\in(E_{f-1}^{\prime},v(D_{f-1}^{\prime}))

s.t.

w_{f-1}^{\prime}\models v(g)
.
Let
w_{f}={[w_{f-1}^{\prime}]}_{v(u)}
.
Clearly,
(E_{f},D_{f})=\overline{\mathit{update}}((E_{f-1}^{\prime},D_{f-1}^{\prime}),u)
is a
\mathsf{point}
–
\mathsf{p}
–PDBM;
as
(E_{f},v(D_{f}))
contains only one clock valuation precisely defined by the fully parametric update
v(u)
, we have
w_{f}\in(E_{f},v(D_{f}))
.
From
4.11
as
w_{f-1}^{\prime}\in(E_{f-1}^{\prime},v(D_{f-1}^{\prime}))
there is a delay
\delta
and a
w_{f-1}\in(E_{f-1},v(D_{f-1}))

s.t.

w_{f-1}^{\prime}=w_{f-1}+\delta
.
Using the induction hypothesis, there is a run

\rho=(\ell_{0},w_{0})\overset{e_{0}}{\longrightarrow}\cdots\overset{e_{f-2}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})
of
v(\mathcal{A})
of length
f-1

s.t.
for all
0\leq i\leq f-1
,
w_{i}\in(E_{i},v(D_{i}))
.
Finally there is an edge
(\ell_{f-1},w_{f-1})\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},w_{f})

and a run
\rho^{\prime}=\rho\overset{e_{f-1}}{\longrightarrow}(\ell_{f},w_{f})
in
v(\mathcal{A})
of length
f

s.t.
for all
0\leq i\leq f
,
w_{i}\in(E_{i},v(D_{i}))
.

–

The case where
e_{f-1}
contains a non parametric guard and a parametric update is similar to the previous one.
